## Part 1. Measurement scripts (run on production, in the in-app browser)

Open `https://lsatprep.study/classroom/drill` signed in as Joshua (he is signed in on that browser). Run each script with the browser's JavaScript tool. Record the output verbatim in the report.

### 1a. Contrast scan

```js
(() => {
  const L = ([r,g,b]) => { const f = c => { c /= 255; return c <= 0.03928 ? c/12.92 : ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4; }; return 0.2126*f(r)+0.7152*f(g)+0.0722*f(b); };
  const parse = s => { const m = (s.match(/[\d.]+/g)||[]).map(Number); return { rgb: m.slice(0,3), a: m.length > 3 ? m[3] : 1 }; };
  const over = (top, a, under) => top.map((c,i) => Math.round(c*a + under[i]*(1-a)));
  const bgOf = el => { let n = el, stack = []; while (n && n.nodeType === 1) { const { rgb, a } = parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor); if (a > 0) stack.push({ rgb, a }); if (a >= 1) break; n = n.parentElement; } let out = [255,255,255]; for (let i = stack.length-1; i >= 0; i--) out = over(stack[i].rgb, stack[i].a, out); return out; };
  const ratio = (a, b) => { const x = L(a), y = L(b); return (Math.max(x,y)+0.05)/(Math.min(x,y)+0.05); };
  const root = document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root') || document.body;
  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
  const fails = [], seen = new Set(); let count = 0, node;
  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {
    const t = node.textContent.trim(); if (!t) continue;
    const el = node.parentElement; if (seen.has(el)) continue; seen.add(el);
    const cs = getComputedStyle(el); if (cs.visibility === 'hidden' || cs.display === 'none') continue;
    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); if (r.width === 0 || r.height === 0) continue;
    const fg = parse(cs.color); const fgRgb = fg.a < 1 ? over(fg.rgb, fg.a, bgOf(el)) : fg.rgb;
    const bg = bgOf(el); const c = ratio(fgRgb, bg);
    const size = parseFloat(cs.fontSize), bold = parseInt(cs.fontWeight) >= 700;
    const large = size >= 24 || (size >= 18.66 && bold);
    const need = large ? 3 : 4.5; count++;
    if (c < need) fails.push({ text: t.slice(0,40), ratio: +c.toFixed(2), need, color: cs.color, bg: `rgb(${bg})`, cls: el.className.toString().slice(0,60) });
  }
  return { checked: count, fails };
})()
```

Caveat: text over a photograph reports the scrim colour, not the photo. For the hero (and Today, until WP-A removes its photo), also zoom the screenshot and confirm by eye that the lightest part of the photo under any text still reads. If unsure, darken the scrim.

### 1b. Target size scan

```js
(() => {
  const els = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root a, .drill-observatory-root button, .drill-observatory-root [role="button"], .drill-observatory-root input, .drill-observatory-root select, .drill-observatory-root [tabindex="0"]')];
  const small = els.filter(e => { const r = e.getBoundingClientRect(); return r.width > 0 && r.height > 0 && r.height < 44; })
    .map(e => ({ text: (e.innerText || e.getAttribute('aria-label') || '').trim().slice(0,30), w: Math.round(e.getBoundingClientRect().width), h: Math.round(e.getBoundingClientRect().height), cls: e.className.toString().slice(0,50) }));
  return { total: els.length, under44: small };
})()
```
Everything on this page must be 44 by height. The active option of the section switch may report oddly because it sits on an absolutely positioned indicator; check it by eye if it appears.

### 1c. Focus ring scan

```js
(() => {
  const els = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root a, .drill-observatory-root button, .drill-observatory-root input, .drill-observatory-root select, .drill-observatory-root [tabindex="0"]')];
  const bad = [];
  for (const e of els) { e.focus({ preventScroll: true }); const cs = getComputedStyle(e); const ok = (cs.outlineStyle !== 'none' && parseFloat(cs.outlineWidth) >= 2) || cs.boxShadow.includes('inset'); if (!ok && document.activeElement === e) bad.push({ text: (e.innerText||e.getAttribute('aria-label')||'').trim().slice(0,30), outline: cs.outline, shadow: cs.boxShadow.slice(0,50) }); }
  document.activeElement.blur();
  return { total: els.length, noRing: bad };
})()
```
Browsers apply `:focus-visible` only on keyboard focus, so this script can over-report. If it lists many, confirm manually: press Tab from the top and screenshot each stop. The manual check is the one that counts.

### 1d. Headings, em-dashes, overflow, width

```js
(() => {
  const hs = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root h1, .drill-observatory-root h2, .drill-observatory-root h3, .drill-observatory-root h4')].map(h => h.tagName + ': ' + h.innerText.trim().slice(0,40));
  const em = (document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root')?.innerText.match(/\u2014/g) || []).length;
  const wide = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root *')].filter(e => e.getBoundingClientRect().right > window.innerWidth + 1).map(e => e.className.toString().slice(0,50)).slice(0,10);
  return { viewport: window.innerWidth, docWidth: document.documentElement.scrollWidth, headings: hs, emDashes: em, pastRightEdge: wide };
})()
```
`docWidth` must equal `viewport`. `pastRightEdge` must be empty. Headings must read h1 then h2s, no skips.

### WP-E. `!important` to zero inside the observatory block

**Why.** 200 `!important` declarations exist to beat shadcn's Card and Button styles. Theming 5 needs the cascade to work without force.

**Steps.**
1. Understand why they are there: `Card` and `Button` from `src/components/ui/` apply Tailwind utility classes (`rounded-lg border bg-card shadow-sm`, `inline-flex h-10 px-4 ...`). Tailwind utilities have single-class specificity; the observatory rules are also mostly single-class, so the author used `!important` to win. The fix is specificity, not force.
2. Wrap every observatory selector in the block (lines ~840 to ~2060) with the root: `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta { ... }` has (0,2,0) and beats any utility. Do this mechanically with a script, not by hand. Write `scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs` that:
   - reads classroom.css,
   - for every rule between the line containing `.drill-observatory-root {` and the line containing `/* end observatory */` (add that comment marker at the end of the block first, after the reduced-motion rule at ~2055),
   - for each selector in a rule's selector list that starts with `.drill-observatory-` and is not already prefixed, prefixes `.drill-observatory-root `,
   - strips ` !important` from every declaration in that range,
   - writes the file back and prints counts.
   Media queries inside the range are handled the same way (the script only touches selector lines and declaration lines; braces stay).
3. Run the script once. Run typecheck and tests. Publish (with WP-F). Then measure: screenshot at 375 and 1280 and compare to the WP-D screenshots side by side. Anything that changed shape means a rule lost to a utility. Find the rule, raise its specificity by one more class (for example `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero .drill-observatory-cta`), never by `!important`.
4. Known hot spots to check first after the script: the CTA (`h-10` and `px-4` from Button), the console start button, the hero Card (`rounded-lg border shadow-sm`), the practice Card, Today's inner Card, the `min-height` on the hero grid.
5. The rules for `.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill ...` (lines ~840 to ~980) are outside the observatory root; leave their `!important`s for this WP but count them separately in the report.
6. Target: `grep -c '!important'` on the file drops from 200 to under 30, and zero of the remaining are inside the observatory block. Record before and after counts.
7. Update the surface brief "Known debt": remove the `!important` line and replace it with one sentence: "Observatory rules are scoped under `.drill-observatory-root` and win by specificity, not `!important`."

**Done when.** Zero `!important` between the observatory markers, page looks identical at 375 and 1280 to the WP-D screenshots, tests pass.

### WP-F. The hub honours the theme toggle

**Why.** The app has a light/dark toggle (`ThemeContext.tsx` adds `.light` to `<html>`; default is dark). The hub renders the same cream stage in both. Theming 5 means the room responds.

**Steps.**
1. Find where the hub's stage tokens are declared: `--observatory-stage-well`, `--observatory-stage-ink`, `--observatory-stage-muted`, `--observatory-stage-hair`, `--observatory-stage-lit` (classroom.css ~line 862 onward, under `.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill, .drill-observatory-root`). Also `--classroom-drill-page` at lines 22 (light, `#fdf8f3`) and 59 (dark, `#121019`).
2. Decide the dark stage: `--classroom-drill-page: #121019` already exists for dark. Add a dark set of stage tokens next to it in the same dark block: `--observatory-stage-well: #1a1822; --observatory-stage-ink: #ece8e1; --observatory-stage-muted: #a39e98; --observatory-stage-hair: rgba(236,232,225,0.12); --observatory-stage-lit: #211f2a`. The cards stay `#08080b` in both themes (they are already dark).
3. Make the observatory block read those tokens from the theme rather than redeclaring them. If the observatory root redeclares `--observatory-stage-*` with literal values, move the declarations up into the light and dark blocks at lines ~22 and ~59 and delete them from the observatory root.
4. The jump row and section switch use `--observatory-stage-*` (via `--qj-*` aliases in questionJump.css and the toggle rules). Confirm they follow.
5. Contrast in dark: stage muted `#a39e98` on `#1a1822` is about 6.2:1, ink is about 12:1. Verify with 1a after publish, in both themes. Toggle the theme from the account menu in the browser; if you cannot find the toggle, ask Joshua where it is.
6. Update the surface brief "The ground": "The stage is cream in light theme and near-black `#121019` in dark; the cards are `#08080b` in both." Remove the dark-toggle line from Known debt.

**Done when.** Toggling the theme changes the stage, the jump row, and the section switch; contrast passes in both; the brief says so.

### WP-G. Fewer labels, less text-shadow, quieter hero

**Why.** Anti-patterns and Aesthetic. The mono uppercase micro-label is used many times on one screen; each one is a decoration when it is not carrying a fact. Text-shadow on every hero line is doing the scrim's job.

**Steps.**
1. Count `drill-observatory-label` usages rendered on the default LR stage (search the three TSX files). List them in the report. Keep: the "Next up" facts line, the date, the "Your practice" and "Also today" card headings, the insight kicker. Remove: any label that repeats the heading above it, and any label on a control (a button says what it does in sentence case).

### WP-C. The phone menu button stops floating over content

**Why.** On phones the menu button is `position: fixed` at 16px/16px, so once the student scrolls it sits on top of "Your practice" and the Today card. This is the one Responsive defect that is a real usability problem.

**Steps.**
1. In `ClassroomLayout.tsx` around line 1019 the button is rendered as a direct child of the root. Wrap it in a bar: `<div className="classroom-mobile-bar">` containing the button and, to its right, a small text label with the section name (there is an `activeSection` value already in scope; render its display label, or "Drill" on the drill root). Keep the button's `aria-label`.
2. In classroom.css, add:
```css
.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar { display: none; }
@media (max-width: 1023px) {
  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {
    align-items: center;
    background: var(--classroom-page);
    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
    display: flex;
    gap: 12px;
    height: 56px;
    padding: 0 12px;
    position: sticky;
    top: 0;
    z-index: 70;
  }
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-mobile-bar { background: var(--classroom-drill-page); }
  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-menu-button { position: static; }
  .classroom-root .classroom-column { padding-top: 16px; }
  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage { padding-top: var(--observatory-space-4); }
}
```
   Check the existing rule at classroom.css ~3114 (`padding: 72px 0 90px`) and the drill stage rules at ~1984 and ~2014 (`padding-top: 72px`). Change those 72px values to 16px, because the bar now takes its own space. Do the same for `.classroom-root-schedule` at ~3119.
3. The bar is sticky, so it stays at the top while scrolling but is part of the flow. Confirm the jump row starts right under it with no overlap at 375.
4. Every other classroom section (Home, Messages, Schedule, Workspace) shares this shell. Open each on production at 375 after publish and screenshot the top. Nothing may overlap the bar. If a section had its own 72px allowance somewhere else, adjust it.
5. The bar text: mono label style is wrong here (this is the shell, not the hub). Use the shell's own label style, `--classroom-meta-text`, 13px, weight 500.

**Done when.** At 375 and 768, scrolled to the bottom of the hub, the menu button is in a bar above the content and covers nothing. All five classroom sections screenshot clean at 375.

Render both spans: `<span className="drill-observatory-date-long">` and `<span className="drill-observatory-date-short">`. In classroom.css: `.drill-observatory-date-short { display: none; }` and inside `@media (min-width: 900px) and (max-width: 1099px) { .drill-observatory-date-long { display: none } .drill-observatory-date-short { display: inline } }`. {"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\components\\classroom\\classroom.css", "old_string": "  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {\n    align-items: center;\n    background: var(--classroom-page);\n    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);\n    display: flex;\n    gap: 12px;\n    height: 56px;", "new_string": "  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {\n    align-items: center;\n    background: var(--classroom-page);\n    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);\n    display: flex;\n    /* A column flex item shrinks when its siblings ask for the full height, and\n       Messages did exactly that: the bar came out 52px instead of 56. */\n    flex: none;\n    gap: 12px;\n    height: 56px;"}

## assistant

I used `!important`, which violates plan rule 10 (the count may only go down). Not needed — the tracking is *inherited* by the span, and any direct declaration on the span beats an inherited value regardless of the parent's `!important`.

## assistant

Reset. Here's where things stand.

## `13bb1dd` was already published — and it shipped a real bug

I checked production directly instead of asking: `.classroom-mobile-bar` was present with the menu button at `position: static`, and both datelines rendered. So WP-C and WP-D were live.

But the five-section sweep the plan requires caught a regression. WP-C added the bar as a third child of `.classroom-root` — which is `display: flex` with `align-items: flex-start`, a **row**. Below the sidebar breakpoint the bar claimed its own column instead of a band across the top:

| Surface at 375 | Stage before | Stage after |
|---|---|---|
| Drill hub | `x:104 w:271` (content 239px) | `x:0 w:375` (content 343px) |
| Home | `x:116 w:259` | `x:0 w:375` |
| Schedule | `x:133 w:242` (calendar column 206px) | `x:0 w:375` (339px) |
| Workspace | `x:194 w:181` | `x:0 w:375` |
| Messages | bar 137px wide | bar 375px |

Every scan passed while this was live — contrast 0 fails, targets 0 under 44, `docWidth` 375, nothing past the right edge. Nothing *overflowed*; the room was just squeezed. Only the screenshot showed it, which is exactly why WP-C step 4 asked for one.

Messages exposed a second-order bug in my first fix: in a column the bar is `flex: 0 1 auto`, so a full-height sibling shrank it to 52px. It needs `flex: none`.

## Also finished WP-D, which had not actually met its criterion

At 1024 the *short* dateline still wrapped: the greeting column is 201px and the line measured 197px of glyphs plus 47px of tracking, so "2026" fell to a second line. Tightening tracking on the span holds it to one line (185px), verified at 900, 1024, and 1099. I did that without `!important` — a value set on the element beats one inherited from the label — so the count stayed at 192, never up.

**Pushed `a82333a`. Ready to publish.** Typecheck clean, 98 classroom tests, 25 mainhub tests, `!important` 192, staged `classroom.css` only.

Two things worth recording:

- **A measurement limitation:** the in-app browser returns a blank image for any *scrolled* screenshot of this page (the capture window doesn't follow scroll; content paints fine at `scrollY=150`). I used DOM geometry and `elementFromPoint` for the scrolled overlap proof instead, which is stronger evidence than eyeballing. RUN3 will say so rather than claim a screenshot I don't have.
- **The plan is wrong on one point:** the theme class is `html.dark` ([classroom.css:62](projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/classroom.css:62)), not the `.light` its Part 0 table claims. WP-F must be written against `html.dark`, as the handoff flagged.

## Why I stopped short of WP-E

I did not start the `!important` rewrite. Publishing a 1,000-line mechanical specificity change *together* with this layout fix would make any visual regression ambiguous — and WP-E's own acceptance test is "looks identical to the WP-D screenshots," which needs a clean published baseline to compare against. Publish `a82333a` first, I'll verify it across the six viewports and the five sections, and that becomes the baseline for E+F.

One decision I'll want your call on when I get there: the `prefers-reduced-motion` block sits inside the observatory range and its `!important`s are load-bearing — an accessibility override that must beat animation declarations, where specificity alone can lose on source order. I plan to keep those and report the count as "zero outside the reduced-motion block" rather than hit a literal zero by weakening a motion-sensitivity guarantee. Say the word if you'd rather I chase the zero.

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\scripts\\observatory-specificity.mjs", "content": "/**\n * Scope the drill hub's CSS under .drill-observatory-root so it wins the\n * cascade by specificity instead of by force.\n *\n * The observatory rules compete with shadcn's Card and Button, which style\n * themselves with Tailwind utilities. A utility is a single class, (0,1,0), and\n * so were most observatory rules, so whoever came last won and the author\n * reached for !important 192 times. Prefixing a rule with the root class makes\n * it (0,2,0), which beats any utility on specificity alone and leaves the\n * cascade working normally for anything that legitimately needs to override.\n *\n * Two things are deliberately left alone:\n *\n *   1. The prefers-reduced-motion block. Its !important is the switch that\n *      turns animation OFF for a reader who asked their OS for less motion. It\n *      has to beat a later, equally specific animation rule, and specificity\n *      cannot promise that. Weakening it to win a counting exercise would trade\n *      an accessibility guarantee for a smaller number.\n *   2. Selectors that do not start with .drill-observatory-, and ones already\n *      scoped to the root. The only one of the former is\n *      .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage at (0,3,0), which\n *      is already stronger than any utility.\n *\n * Idempotent: running it twice changes nothing the second time.\n *\n *   node scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs [--check]\n *\n * --check exits 1 if the file would change, for use as a guard.\n */\nimport fs from \"node:fs\";\nimport postcss from \"postcss\";\n\nconst FILE = \"src/components/classroom/classroom.css\";\nconst ROOT_CLASS = \".drill-observatory-root\";\nconst PREFIX = `${ROOT_CLASS} `;\nconst END_MARKER = \"end observatory\";\nconst check = process.argv.includes(\"--check\");\n\nconst css = fs.readFileSync(FILE, \"utf8\");\nconst ast = postcss.parse(css);\n\n/* The block runs from the rule that declares the root's tokens to the\n   `end observatory` comment. Both are required: guessing the boundary by line\n   number would silently rewrite whatever the file grows next. */\nconst startIdx = ast.nodes.findIndex(\n  (n) => n.type === \"rule\" && n.selector.trim() === ROOT_CLASS,\n);\nconst endIdx = ast.nodes.findIndex(\n  (n) => n.type === \"comment\" && n.text.trim() === END_MARKER,\n);\n\nif (startIdx < 0) throw new Error(`Could not find the \"${ROOT_CLASS}\" rule that opens the block.`);\nif (endIdx < 0) throw new Error(`Could not find the \"/* ${END_MARKER} */\" marker that closes the block.`);\nif (endIdx < startIdx) throw new Error(\"The end marker sits before the opening rule.\");\n\nconst stats = { rules: 0, selectorsPrefixed: 0, importantStripped: 0, importantKept: 0 };\n\nconst scopeSelector = (selector) => {\n  const s = selector.trim();\n  if (!s.startsWith(\".drill-observatory-\")) return s; // already stronger, or not ours\n  if (s === ROOT_CLASS || s.startsWith(PREFIX)) return s; // already scoped\n  stats.selectorsPrefixed += 1;\n  return PREFIX + s;\n};\n\nconst visit = (node, inReducedMotion) => {\n  if (node.type === \"rule\") {\n    stats.rules += 1;\n    node.selectors = node.selectors.map(scopeSelector);\n    node.walkDecls((decl) => {\n      if (!decl.important) return;\n      if (inReducedMotion) {\n        stats.importantKept += 1;\n        return;\n      }\n      decl.important = false;\n      stats.importantStripped += 1;\n    });\n    return;\n  }\n  if (node.type === \"atrule\") {\n    const reduced = inReducedMotion || /prefers-reduced-motion/.test(node.params || \"\");\n    node.each((child) => visit(child, reduced));\n  }\n};\n\nfor (let i = startIdx; i < endIdx; i += 1) visit(ast.nodes[i], false);\n\nconst out = ast.toString();\nconst changed = out !== css;\n\nconst countImportant = (text) => (text.match(/!important/g) || []).length;\n\nconsole.log(`observatory block: nodes ${startIdx}..${endIdx}`);\nconsole.log(`  rules visited        ${stats.rules}`);\nconsole.log(`  selectors scoped     ${stats.selectorsPrefixed}`);\nconsole.log(`  !important stripped  ${stats.importantStripped}`);\nconsole.log(`  !important kept      ${stats.importantKept} (prefers-reduced-motion)`);\nconsole.log(`  file !important      ${countImportant(css)} -> ${countImportant(out)}`);\n\nif (check) {\n  if (changed) {\n    console.error(\"FAIL: the observatory block is not scoped. Run without --check.\");\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n  console.log(\"OK: already scoped.\");\n  process.exit(0);\n}\n\nif (!changed) {\n  console.log(\"No change; already scoped.\");\n} else {\n  fs.writeFileSync(FILE, out);\n  console.log(`Wrote ${FILE}.`);\n}\n"}

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

  "product": "# Product\r\n\r\n<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->\r\n\r\n## Scope\r\n\r\nApp-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.\r\n\r\nA rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.\r\n\r\nThe repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.\r\n\r\n## Platform\r\n\r\nweb\r\n\r\n## Users\r\n\r\n**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.\r\n\r\n**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.\r\n\r\n**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.\r\n\r\n## Product Purpose\r\n\r\nLSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.\r\n\r\nFor a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.\r\n\r\nFor Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.\r\n\r\nSuccess is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.\r\n\r\n## Positioning\r\n\r\n**A real coach is in it.**\r\n\r\nThe claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.\r\n\r\n## Operating Context\r\n\r\nThe platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.\r\n\r\n- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.\r\n- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).\r\n- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.\r\n- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.\r\n- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.\r\n\r\n## Capabilities and Constraints\r\n\r\nConfirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teacher workspace.\r\n\r\nTechnical constraints:\r\n\r\n- React, TypeScript, Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui and Radix primitives, React Router, TanStack Query, Supabase.\r\n- One motion library (`framer-motion`). One primary icon library (`lucide-react`). New work composes existing primitives before introducing a new dependency.\r\n- Deployment is not a side effect of pushing to `main`. Production at lsatprep.study requires an explicit publish step.\r\n- Database migrations in `supabase/migrations/` are an archive, not an applied ledger. Production schema changes go through their own path, and UI must not assume a table exists because a migration file does.\r\n\r\nTerminology is not defined here. It is defined per bounded context and mapped by `CONTEXT-MAP.md`. A word can carry different weight in different contexts, and collapsing them has already caused real bugs (a **flag** is not a **wrong answer**; **Set** and **Position** were separated for this reason).\r\n\r\n## Brand Commitments\r\n\r\n- **Name:** LSAT U, operating under Aspiring Attorneys. Production domain lsatprep.study.\r\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect reads.** Joshua does not type them. A stray em-dash reads as machine-written and costs trust. This binds computed copy, utility copy, and authored copy alike.\r\n- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a \"coach voice, not industry voice\" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua's decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.\r\n\r\n## Evidence on Hand\r\n\r\nReal:\r\n\r\n- A live production deploy

## user

Tool result:
> **Additional context needed**: performance constraints.

Analyze a feature and strategically add animations and micro-interactions that enhance understanding, provide feedback, and create delight.

---

## Register

Brand: orchestrated page-load sequences, staggered reveals, scroll-driven animation. Motion is part of the voice; one well-rehearsed entrance beats scattered micro-interactions.

Product: 150–250 ms on most transitions. Motion conveys state — feedback, reveal, loading, transitions between views. No page-load choreography; users are in a task and won't wait for it.

---

## Assess Animation Opportunities

Analyze where motion would improve the experience:

1. **Identify static areas**:
   - **Missing feedback**: Actions without visual acknowledgment (button clicks, form submission, etc.)
   - **Jarring transitions**: Instant state changes that feel abrupt (show/hide, page loads, route changes)
   - **Unclear relationships**: Spatial or hierarchical relationships that aren't obvious
   - **Lack of delight**: Functional but joyless interactions
   - **Missed guidance**: Opportunities to direct attention or explain behavior

2. **Understand the context**:
   - What's the personality? (Playful vs serious, energetic vs calm)
   - What's the performance budget? (Mobile-first? Complex page?)
   - Who's the audience? (Motion-sensitive users? Power users who want speed?)
   - What matters most? (One hero animation vs many micro-interactions?)

If any of these are unclear from the codebase, STOP and use Codex's structured user-input/question tool when available; if unavailable, ask directly in chat to clarify what you cannot infer.

**CRITICAL**: Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. Always provide non-animated alternatives for users who need them.

## Plan Animation Strategy

Create a purposeful animation plan:

- **Hero moment**: What's the ONE signature animation? (Page load? Hero section? Key interaction?)
- **Feedback layer**: Which interactions need acknowledgment?
- **Transition layer**: Which state changes need smoothing?
- **Delight layer**: Where can we surprise and delight?

**IMPORTANT**: One well-orchestrated experience beats scattered animations everywhere. Focus on high-impact moments.

## Implement Animations

Add motion systematically across these categories:

### Entrance Animations
- **Page load choreography**: Stagger element reveals (100-150ms delays), fade + slide combinations
- **Hero section**: Dramatic entrance for primary content (scale, parallax, or creative effects)
- **Content reveals**: Scroll-triggered animations using intersection observer
- **Modal/drawer entry**: Smooth slide + fade, backdrop fade, focus management

### Micro-interactions
- **Button feedback**:
  - Hover: Subtle scale (1.02-1.05), color shift, shadow increase
  - Click: Quick scale down then up (0.95 → 1), ripple effect
  - Loading: Spinner or pulse state
- **Form interactions**:
  - Input focus: Border color transition, slight scale or glow
  - Validation: Shake on error, check mark on success, smooth color transitions
- **Toggle switches**: Smooth slide + color transition (200-300ms)
- **Checkboxes/radio**: Check mark animation, ripple effect
- **Like/favorite**: Scale + rotation, particle effects, color transition

### State Transitions
- **Show/hide**: Fade + slide (not instant), appropriate timing (200-300ms)
- **Expand/collapse**: Height transition with overflow handling, icon rotation
- **Loading states**: Skeleton screen fades, spinner animations, progress bars
- **Success/error**: Color transitions, icon animations, gentle scale pulse
- **Enable/disable**: Opacity transitions, cursor changes

### Navigation & Flow
- **Page transitions**: Crossfade between routes, shared element transitions
- **Tab switching**: Slide indicator, content fade/slide
- **Carousel/slider**: Smooth transforms, snap points, momentum
- **Scroll effects**: Parallax layers, sticky headers with state changes, scroll progress indicators

### Feedback & Guidance
- **Hover hints**: Tooltip fade-ins, cursor changes, element highlights
- **Drag & drop**: Lift effect (shadow + scale), drop zone highlights, smooth repositioning
- **Copy/paste**: Brief highlight flash on paste, "copied" confirmation
- **Focus flow**: Highlight path through form or workflow

### Delight Moments
- **Empty states**: Subtle floating animations on illustrations
- **Completed actions**: Confetti, check mark flourish, success celebrations
- **Easter eggs**: Hidden interactions for discovery
- **Contextual animation**: Weather effects, time-of-day themes, seasonal touches

## Technical Implementation

Use appropriate techniques for each animation:

### Timing & Easing

**Durations by purpose:**
- **100-150ms**: Instant feedback (button press, toggle)
- **200-300ms**: State changes (hover, menu open)
- **300-500ms**: Layout changes (accordion, modal)
- **500-800ms**: Entrance animations (page load)

**Easing curves (use these, not CSS defaults):**
```css
/* Recommended - natural deceleration */
--ease-out-quart: cubic-bezier(0.25, 1, 0.5, 1);    /* Smooth, refined */
--ease-out-quint: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);   /* Slightly snappier */
--ease-out-expo: cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1);     /* Confident, decisive */

/* AVOID - feel dated and tacky */
/* bounce: cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1); */
/* elastic: cubic-bezier(0.68, -0.6, 0.32, 1.6); */
```

**Exit animations are faster than entrances.** Use ~75% of enter duration.

### CSS Animations
```css
/* Prefer for simple, declarative animations */
- transitions for state changes
- @keyframes for complex sequences
- transform and opacity for reliable movement
- blur, filters, masks, clip paths, shadows, and color shifts for premium atmospheric effects when verified smooth
```

### JavaScript Animation
```javascript
/* Use for complex, interactive animations */
- Web Animations API for programmatic control
- Framer Motion for React
- GSAP for complex sequences
```

### Performance
- **Motion materials**: Use transform/opacity for reliable movement, but use blur, filters, masks, shadows, and color shifts when they materially improve the effect
- **Layout safety**: Avoid casual animation of layout-driving properties (`width`, `height`, `top`, `left`, margins)
- **will-change**: Add sparingly for known expensive animations
- **Bound expensive effects**: Keep blur/filter/shadow areas small or isolated, use `contain` where appropriate
- **Monitor FPS**: Ensure 60fps on target devices

### Accessibility
```css
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  * {
    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
  }
}
```

**NEVER**:
- Use bounce or elastic easing curves—they feel dated and draw attention to the animation itself
- Animate layout properties casually (`width`, `height`, `top`, `left`, margins) when transform, FLIP, or grid-based techniques would work
- Use durations over 500ms for feedback—it feels laggy
- Animate without purpose—every animation needs a reason
- Ignore `prefers-reduced-motion`—this is an accessibility violation
- Animate everything—animation fatigue makes interfaces feel exhausting
- Block interaction during animations unless intentional

## Verify Quality

Test animations thoroughly:

- **Smooth at 60fps**: No jank on target devices
- **Feels natural**: Easing curves feel organic, not robotic
- **Appropriate timing**: Not too fast (jarring) or too slow (laggy)
- **Reduced motion works**: Animations disabled or simplified appropriately
- **Doesn't block**: Users can interact during/after animations
- **Adds value**: Makes interface clearer or more delightful

Remember: Motion should enhance understanding and provide feedback, not just add decoration. Animate with purpose, respect performance constraints, and always consider accessibility. Great animation is invisible - it just makes everything feel right.

## user

Tool result:
# Surface brief: the drill hub

Route: `/classroom/drill` (also `/practice`, which redirects). Code: `src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx`, `src/components/mainhub/`, the `.drill-observatory-*` block of `src/components/classroom/classroom.css`, `src/components/drill/SectionToggle.tsx`. Wrapper: `.classroom-root-drill` and `.drill-observatory-root`.

Written 2026-09-08 from the impeccable audit and critique, after Joshua's two decisions: Today's assigned item leads the hero, and this hub is a sanctioned scoped world with its own record. This file is that record. DESIGN.md's Workroom rules do not apply inside the wrapper; the rules below do.

## What the page is for

A paying client logs in to do what Joshua assigned. The page tells them that first, in the hero, with one button. Everything else on the page (adaptive drill, the builder, the question jump, insights) is the fallback for a student with nothing assigned, or the second thing after the assignment. Product principle 2 (compute the next step, do not present a grid) is the whole layout brief.

Order of the page, top to bottom: utilities (jump, search, LR/RC), the hero (greeting, Next up, one CTA, one quiet alternative, the builder console), the practice card (insights, Analytics, Journal), Today (the queue behind the hero's item).

## The ground

The stage is cream (`--classroom-drill-page`). The cards on it are near-black (`--observatory-surface-card` `#08080b`). One card, the hero, carries a photograph (the horizon), and it is the only decoration on the page. Today was flattened to the same near-black surface as the practice card on 2026-09-08; two photographs on one screen read as a template. No accent colour. No gradients. No grid textures.

Controls that sit on the stage itself (the jump row, the section switch) are inked for the stage: `--observatory-stage-*`. Controls on a card are inked ivory: the `--observatory-ink*` ladder. The 2026-09-08 audit found the jump row inked for the cards while sitting on the stage, at 1.07:1. Tone is decided by what a control sits on, never by what is near it.

## Ink and contrast

WCAG AA is a defect gate here as everywhere. Measured against `#08080b`, the ivory ladder passes for text from `--observatory-ink-50` (4.9:1) upward. The steps below it (0.38, 0.42, 0.45, 0.48) were removed on 2026-09-08; they were carrying labels at 3.2:1 to 4.2:1. Nothing that carries text goes below `ink-50`. Hairlines, rings and skeleton bars use their own tokens and may be fainter.

On the stage: `--observatory-stage-ink` 12.9:1, `--observatory-stage-muted` 5.3:1 on `--observatory-stage-well`.

Over the photographs, text sits on a scrim (`::before` on the hero and on Today's inner surface). The scrim is mandatory; the photographs have bright crops.

## Type

Three families, on purpose, one job each:

- Fraunces, weight 400, `opsz` 60: the greeting only (`h1`). It is the one serif line on the page. Insight values and Today titles use it at 20 to 22px as the page's second display voice. Nothing else.
- JetBrains Mono, 10 to 11px, tracked 0.16 to 0.18em, uppercase: micro-labels and chips. Never a sentence.
- Outfit: everything else. Body 13.5 to 14px, the Next up title 19px semibold, the console heading fluid.

## Depth

Hairline rings (`0 0 0 1px`) separate surfaces. Shadows are quiet: `0 12px 32px` at 12 to 16 percent, not the 60px at 25 to 35 percent the page shipped with. Glass appears only on the hero console. It is the one `backdrop-filter` on the page, at `blur(12px)`, and it is there because it sits over the photograph. A glass panel anywhere else, including on Today, is a defect.

## Controls

- 44px minimum by height, met by the control: the CTA (46px), the console button (46px), the section switch options (44px), selects (44px), secondary links and insight actions (44px). No pseudo-element hit areas.
- Focus: a 2px solid ivory outline (`--observatory-focus`), offset 3px, on every pill control. Full-bleed rows inside a clipped container use the inset variant (`.drill-observatory-focus-inset`). On the stage, a 2px `--observatory-stage-ink` outline. No control removes an outline without one of these.
- Hover: a brightness or colour change. Rails and tiles may lift 2px; the primary CTA never moves.
- Motion: `--observatory-motion` (160ms) for state; the section switch's indicator is a spring. Transform and opacity only. The RC flip's height snaps. `prefers-reduced-motion` stops everything.

## Doors

One door to the builder: the hero console. The practice card does not repeat it. An insight may offer a contextual action (open analytics, open the journal, drill a weak type) because it is a response to real data, not a menu item.

While a stage (builder, analytics) is open, the hero is not rendered. The stage carries its own title and Cancel.

## Copy

No em-dashes anywhere a student can read, including placeholders. Buttons say what they do ("Start", "Resume drill", "Set up an LR drill"). "Smart Drill" is the product's name for the adaptive drill and stays, with the sentence before it saying what it does. The date reads the same as Classroom Home: `Tuesday · September 8 · 2026`.

## Known debt (not fixed on 2026-09-08)

- Observatory rules are scoped under `.drill-observatory-root` and win by specificity, not `!important`. Run `node scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs` after adding a rule to the block; `--check` fails if anything is unscoped. The four `!important` that remain are in the `prefers-reduced-motion` block, where the override has to beat a later animation rule and specificity cannot promise that. Rules for portalled content (`.drill-observatory-launcher-panel`) live outside the `/* end observatory */` marker on purpose: they render outside the root and must not be scoped to it.
- The hub ignores the app's dark toggle: the stage is cream either way. Acceptable while the stage is documented as cream here.
- `.drill-observatory-practice-tile` and `PracticeTile` are still exported for the RC flip and are no longer used on the LR default stage.

## assistant

Two constraints bind here. The register says **"No page-load choreography; users are in a task and won't wait for it"** — so no staggered entrance reveals, which is the obvious pretty thing. And the surface brief already fixes the vocabulary: 160ms for state, transform and opacity only, the primary CTA never moves.

Let me audit what motion actually exists before adding any.

Its first 40% barely moves, which is exactly wrong for feedback: a hover should respond instantly then settle. That's the highest-value change on the page. Two measured findings, and one of them matters for WP-E.

The curve numbers are stark. At 10% of the duration Material's curve has covered **2.6%** of the distance; ease-out-quart has covered **34.7%**. At 25%: 23.7% vs 68.9%. On a 160ms hover that means nothing visible has happened for the first ~40ms.

Second, the jump-row selects report `transition-duration: 0s` — the first controls on the page have no feedback at all.

But the CTA reports `0.2s` on `background-color, border-color, color, box-shadow, transform` — which is *not* what the CSS declares. A Tailwind utility is currently winning. Checking, because WP-E changes who wins.

.qf-select {
  appearance: none;
  background: var(--qf-surface);
  background-image: linear-gradient(45deg, transparent 50%, currentColor 50%),

  font-size: 13px;
  min-width: 0;
  padding: 9px 28px 9px 11px;
  width: 100%;
}

.qf-select:disabled {

=== qj tone overrides ===
.qj {
  --qj-ink: #332f2c;
  --qj-muted: #6b6662;
  --qj-sunk: #fbf8f3;
  --qj-hair: rgba(68, 64, 61, 0.09);
  --qj-ui: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;

  /* The field rules (.qf-field-label, .qf-select) are the finder's and read
     --qf-* tokens, which only exist under .qf. Outside it they resolved to
     nothing and the row inherited its host's ink: ivory on the drill hub's
     cream stage, at 1.06:1. The jump row declares its own values under the
     names the rules actually use. */
  --qf-ink: var(--qj-ink);
  --qf-muted: var(--qj-muted);
  --qf-surface: #ffffff;
  --qf-hair-08: var(--qj-hair);
  --qf-accent: #ff8906;
  --qf-ui: var(--qj-ui);
  --qf-mono: "JetBrains Mono", ui-monospace, monospace;

  width: 100%;
}

.qj[data-tone="dark"] {
  --qj-ink: #f5f1ec;
  --qj-muted: #8b837b;
  --qj-sunk: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.03);
  --qj-hair: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.1);
  --qf-surface: #17161c;
}

.qj-row {
  align-items: end;

  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--qj-hair);
  display: grid;

{"command": "git commit -F - <<'EOF'\nGive every control on the hub a pointer answer, and an ease-out curve\n\nTwo measured defects, not decoration.\n\nThe curve. --observatory-motion used cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1), an\nease-in-out. A curve that eases in spends its opening barely moving, which is\nright for something leaving the screen and wrong for a control answering a\npointer: at 10% of a 160ms transition it has covered 2.6% of the distance and\nat 25% only 23.7%, so nothing visible happens for the first 40ms and the hub\nreads as slow. The quart ease-out covers 34.7% and 68.9% at those points. It\nis now --observatory-ease, used by the state token and the stage entrance.\n\nThe jump row. The three selects are the first controls a student meets on the\nhub and had no hover state at all: :disabled and :focus-visible existed, and\nnothing answered a pointer, against the surface brief's own rule. They now\nstrengthen their ring, mixed from --qf-ink so it reads in both the light row\nand the dark tone. The reduced-motion block gains .qj: the row was covered\nonly because it happens to sit inside .drill-observatory-root, which is\nincidental and would fall away if the row were used anywhere else.\n\nThree transition lists also gained background-color, because their :hover\nchanges it and the list named only transform. That was masked until now by a\nTailwind utility outranking the rule; once the previous commit let the rules\nwin on specificity, those hovers would have snapped instead of blending. The\nCTA, the practice tile and the insight action.\n\nSplit commit, worth knowing: the classroom.css half of this change was swept\ninto 70056d3 by a concurrent session's broad git add, so that commit carries\nmotion work its message does not mention.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1", "description": "Commit the motion pass"}

## assistant

Viewport reset. Here's where things stand.

## First, the thing I said badly

Plainly: browsers have a "reduce motion" accessibility setting for people who get motion sick. The hub has a rule that switches all its animation off when that setting is on. That off-switch is written with `!important` — CSS's "force this" keyword.

The plan told me to remove every `!important` from the hub. Removing those four would mean the off-switch can lose to an animation rule that comes later in the file, so someone who asked their computer for less motion might still get it.

So I removed 188 and kept 4. The count went **192 → 4**, and the 4 that remain are only that off-switch. That was the decision I was asking you to confirm, and I've now made it that way.

## The push rule — you're right, and I'd already broken it

My push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, which carried another session's commit `70056d3` along with mine. Saved as memory: check `git log origin/main..HEAD` before pushing and stop if anything there isn't mine.

Related, and you should know it: that same session's broad `git add` swept **my** uncommitted motion edits into *its* commit `70056d3`, so that commit contains motion work its message doesn't mention. I couldn't unpick it without rewriting their commit, so I said so in mine instead. There's now another of their commits, `292e343`, sitting unpushed — under the new rule I've left it alone.

## The motion work: two measured defects, no decoration

I checked before adding anything, and most of what I expected to be missing already existed — the section switch is a proper framer-motion spring, both skeletons already pulse, there are 14 hovers and 6 press states. So this was a correction pass.

**The easing curve was the wrong family.** The hub used `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)`, an ease-in-out. A curve that eases *in* barely moves at the start, which is right for something leaving the screen and wrong for a control answering a pointer. Measured on both curves:

| Elapsed | Old curve | Ease-out quart |
|---|---|---|
| 10% (16ms) | 2.6% | 34.7% |
| 25% (40ms) | 23.7% | 68.9% |

Nothing visible happened for the first ~40ms of every hover. That's why it read slow.

**The three jump-row selects had no hover state at all** — `:disabled` and `:focus-visible` existed, nothing answered a pointer, against the brief's own rule. They now strengthen their ring, mixed from the ink token so it reads in both the light row and the dark tone.

**And WP-E would have broken three hovers.** The CTA, practice tile and insight action change `background` on hover, but their transition lists named only `transform`. That was masked because a Tailwind utility outranked the rule — the moment WP-E let the authored rules win on specificity, those three hovers would have snapped. Caught by auditing every hover against its transition list, before anyone saw it.

Verified: `color-mix` resolves to a real oklab value in-browser, the nested `var()` motion token substitutes, the select's transition computes to `box-shadow 0.16s cubic-bezier(0.25, 1, 0.5, 1)`. I could not get the harness to produce a real CSS `:hover`, so I verified that chain through the CSSOM instead — rule parses, element matches everything but `:hover`, value resolves — rather than claiming a hover I didn't see.

Typecheck clean, 17 files / 179 tests passing, production build succeeds, `!important` 4, `--check` confirms the block stays scoped.

## What I need from you

**Publish.** Pushed and waiting: `3eb6803` (WP-E) and `5fd1074` (motion).

When you confirm, I run the check I set up for it: before pushing WP-E I captured a computed-style fingerprint of the live published hub — 200 elements × 50 properties at 375 and 1280, stored in the browser. WP-E is supposed to change *nothing* visually, so I diff against that and any element that moved shows up by name. That's the "looks identical" test the plan asks for, done numerically, since this pane can't screenshot a scrolled page.

Then WP-F is next, written against `html.dark` — the plan's claim that the theme class is `.light` is wrong.

## assistant

All 136 box diffs are the same artifact: my baseline was captured mid-`classroom-stage-enter`, so 119 boxes are exactly 4px up and the other 17 add sub-pixel reflow. The real changes are three groups, and they're all the authored rules finally winning:

- **fontFamily ×5** — section toggle Outfit → JetBrains Mono (the brief says chips are mono; a utility was beating the rule)
- **color ×9 + borderTopColor ×2** — `rgb(18,18,18)` → ivory on the hero card and console (shadcn's `text-card-foreground` was winning on a near-black card)
- **width ×6, marginLeft, gap 8→9px** — reflow from the font change and the CTA's own gap winning

The color flip is the one that could hurt, so let me prove nothing became unreadable.

## assistant

Now I have the real diagnosis, and the plan's WP-F is built on a false premise:

- `:root` = dark palette; `.light` class = light palette; default is light
- Dark is signalled by the **absence** of `.light`
- classroom.css keys its entire dark palette on `html.dark` — which nothing in the app ever sets

So the classroom's dark palette is dead code. Switch to dark and the app chrome goes dark while the whole classroom stays cream. Adding dark stage tokens under `html.dark`, as WP-F instructs, would be dead on arrival too.

Let me test whether the already-authored dark palette actually holds up, by simulating the target state on production.

### WP-G. Fewer labels, less text-shadow, quieter hero

**Why.** Anti-patterns and Aesthetic. The mono uppercase micro-label is used many times on one screen; each one is a decoration when it is not carrying a fact. Text-shadow on every hero line is doing the scrim's job.

**Steps.**
1. Count `drill-observatory-label` usages rendered on the default LR stage (search the three TSX files). List them in the report. Keep: the "Next up" facts line, the date, the "Your practice" and "Also today" card headings, the insight kicker. Remove: any label that repeats the heading above it, and any label on a control (a button says what it does in sentence case).
2. Hero text-shadows: with the scrim doing its job, reduce `text-shadow` on `.drill-observatory-greeting h1` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.35)` and on `.drill-observatory-greeting p`, `.drill-observatory-next-title`, `.drill-observatory-quiet-link` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`. Then re-run 1a and check by eye at the lightest crop of the photo. If any hero text is hard to read, darken the scrim by 0.05 alpha rather than restoring the shadow.
3. The quiet link underline: `text-decoration-color: rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.55)` (was 0.35).
4. The CTA's `box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` (classroom.css ~1249): reduce to `0 6px 20px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`.
5. Screenshot the hero at 1280 before and after; put both in the report.

**Done when.** Label count on the LR default stage is at most 6, shadows reduced, contrast still 0 fails.

### WP-H. Copy pass in the teaching voice

**Why.** Critique 2 is about the student's words, not the product's. Read `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` first.

**Steps.**
1. List every string a student sees on the hub (hero, console, practice, Today, jump row, search trigger, section switch, loading and error states). Put the list in the report with a column for the new text.
2. Rules for the new text: sentence case on buttons and headings; the verb first on buttons; say what the thing does before naming it; no "Smart" as a bare adjective without the sentence explaining it; no "instead" links that assume the student rejected the first option.
3. Specific changes:
   - Quiet link "Start a Smart Drill instead" becomes "Or practice your weakest types". RC: "Or read one passage now".
   - Fallback CTA "Start Smart Drill" becomes "Practice your weakest types". RC "Start One Passage" becomes "Read one passage".
   - Fallback paragraph: "Start with the question types you miss most, or build a drill around exactly what you want to practice." RC: "Read one passage now, untimed, with the answers after it. Or build a set of passages around what you want to read."
   - "Set up an LR drill" stays. "Set up an RC drill" stays.
   - "Analytics" and "Journal" links stay.
   - Insight action labels: check each in `insightSlides.ts` against rule 2. Rewrite any that names a screen instead of an outcome ("Open analytics" becomes "See where you lose points").
   - Today "Also today" stays. Its empty line stays.
   - Jump row placeholder stays ("PT101.S2.Q1" form).
4. The heading in the console "Build the drill you need." stays; it is the one door and it says what it does.
5. Update tests that assert the old strings (`AcademyMainhub.test.tsx` looks for "Start Smart Drill" and similar; search the three test files for each old string).
6. Add the new strings to `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` under a dated heading so future work does not revert them.

**Done when.** Every string in the list has been checked, tests updated and green, the corrections log has the entry.

### WP-I. Control and freedom: Escape, back, section memory

**Steps.**
1. Escape closes a stage. In `AcademyMainhub.tsx`, where `stage` is read from the URL and `closeStage` exists (~line 560), add a `useEffect` that listens for `keydown` on `window`; if `event.key === "Escape"` and `stage !== "default"` and the event target is not inside an open dialog or a text input with content, call `closeStage()`. Clean up the listener.
2. Browser back returns to the hub. Check that opening a stage uses `navigate` with a new history entry (not `replace: true`), so back works. If it uses replace, change it to push. Test by opening the builder on production, pressing the browser back button, and confirming the hub returns with the same section.
3. Section memory. When the student switches LR/RC, persist the choice in `localStorage` under `lsatu.drillHub.section`. On mount, if the URL has no `section` param, read it and apply it. Wrap the storage calls in `try/catch`.
4. Cancel in the builder keeps the student's choices for the session: if `QuestionPicker` already holds its state in a store or URL, leave it; if it resets on Cancel, note that in the report (do not build persistence for the builder in this plan).
5. Tests: one test each for Escape closing the stage and for section memory (mock `localStorage`).

**Done when.** Escape works on both stages, back works, the section is remembered across reloads, tests pass.

### WP-J. Consistency: a type scale, one icon size, one radius set

**Steps.**
1. Grep the three TSX files and the observatory block for font sizes: `text-\[`, `font-size:`. List every distinct value. There are at least 12.5px, 13.5px, 14px, 15px, 19px, 20px, 22px, and several clamps.
2. Define a scale as tokens on `.drill-observatory-root`: `--observatory-text-xs: 11px` (mono labels), `--observatory-text-sm: 13px`, `--observatory-text-base: 14px`, `--observatory-text-md: 16px`, `--observatory-text-lg: 19px`, `--observatory-text-xl: 22px`, plus the existing display token. Map every literal to the nearest token. 12.5 becomes sm, 13.5 becomes base, 15 becomes md.
3. Arrow icons: the hero CTA uses 14px (`h-3.5`), the console 16px (`h-4`), insights 13px. Make them all 14px with `strokeWidth={2}`.
4. Radii: list `border-radius` values in the block; there should be exactly three tokens (panel, inner, control) plus `999px` for pills. Replace any literal.
5. Spacing: the block already has `--observatory-space-*`. Replace any literal `px` margin or padding in the block that is not from the scale, unless it is a 1px hairline.
6. Screenshot before and after at 1280. Differences should be sub-pixel.

**Done when.** No literal font-size in the block or the three TSX files outside the tokens, icons one size, radii from tokens.

### WP-K. Error prevention

**Steps.**
1. Jump row (`QuestionJump.tsx`): the three selects (PT, section, question) should never submit an impossible combination. Confirm the question select is disabled until a section is chosen and its options come from the chosen PT and section. If the row has a Go button, disable it until all three are chosen, and add `aria-describedby` pointing at a one-line hint "Choose a PrepTest, then a section, then a question" that shows when the button is disabled. If the row navigates on the third select without a button, confirm that and leave it.
2. Hero CTA: it is `disabled` when `!primary.route && !primary.onOpen`. A disabled button with no reason is a trap. When disabled, render beneath it, in `drill-observatory-ink-60` at text-sm: the true reason. Read `useTodayFocus.ts` to see when route and onOpen are both empty and write the sentence that matches (for example "This item opens from your Classroom home.").
3. Section switch: switching LR to RC while a stage is open should not lose the stage. Check the URL handling; if switching resets `stage`, keep it.
4. Builder: not in scope beyond what the console opens. Note anything you see for later.

**Done when.** No control on the hub can be activated into a dead end without a sentence saying why.

### WP-L. Flexibility

**Steps.**
1. Keyboard shortcut hint without the badge Joshua removed: on the search trigger add `aria-keyshortcuts="Meta+K Control+K"` and a `title="Search questions (Ctrl K)"`. No visible badge.
2. Resume last drill: `useTodayFocus.ts` already knows about an in-progress drill if one exists (status "Resume drill"). If the student has an unfinished drill and no assignment, the hero CTA should be "Resume your drill" with the fallback link beneath. Read the hook; if that case already leads, confirm and note it; if not, add it as the primary when `items` is empty and a resumable drill exists. Do not fabricate a resume state; only use what the hook already loads.
3. Section memory (WP-I) counts here too.
4. Power path: pressing `/` while no input is focused opens the search. Add this in the same `keydown` effect as Escape.

**Done when.** Shortcuts documented in ARIA, `/` opens search, resume leads when it applies, nothing new is fabricated.

### WP-M. Error recovery

**Steps.**
1. List every data source the hub reads: `useTodayFocus` (assignments), `useStudentAnalytics` (insights), the question bank for the launcher, RC history. For each, find what the UI does when the request fails.
2. Each failure gets: one plain sentence saying what did not load, and a "Try again" button that refetches (TanStack Query exposes `refetch`; the launcher already has "Reload the question bank"). Use `drill-observatory-insight-link` styling for the button so it is 44px and has a focus ring.
3. Today: if assignments fail, the hero must not pretend there is nothing assigned. Render the fallback paragraph plus the sentence "Your assigned work did not load. Try again." with the button. `useTodayFocus` returns `hasError`; use it.
4. Insights: if analytics fail, the practice card shows the sentence and button in place of the list, not the streak slide.
5. Offline: if `navigator.onLine` is false at mount, show one line at the top of the hero: "You are offline. Your last loaded work is shown." Only if the app already has an online listener pattern elsewhere; otherwise skip and note.
6. Tests: mock a failed `useTodayFocus` (hasError true) and assert the sentence and button render.

**Done when.** Every source has a failure sentence and a retry, verified in tests; no failure renders an empty or misleading state.

### WP-N. Help

**Why.** Critique 10 was 2. Nothing on the hub explains what an adaptive drill is, what Today means, or how to get help. Three small pieces fix it without adding a documentation page.

**Steps.**
1. Inline disclosure under the fallback paragraph (no assignment): a `<details>` styled as a quiet link, summary "How the adaptive drill picks questions". Body, three sentences: "It looks at the question types you miss most often and pulls from those first. Each set is ten questions. Your results feed the next set." Confirm the ten with the drill code (`/drill` adaptive mode, search for the set size); if it is not ten, write the true number. If you cannot find it, leave the count out.
2. Under the hero when an assignment leads, add one line in `drill-observatory-ink-60` after the facts: "Assigned work shows here first. Your own practice is below." Show it only the first three times the student sees the hub with an assignment; count in `localStorage` under `lsatu.drillHub.assignedHintSeen`. Wrap in `try/catch`.
3. Help link: the hub has `MainhubHelpControl` (a message thread to Joshua). Make sure it is reachable from the hub with a visible control labelled "Ask Joshua". If it is already in the dock, confirm the label and its 44px size; if it is not, do not add a new floating button. Note the state in the report.
4. Tests: the disclosure renders in the no-assignment state and is absent when an assignment leads.

The horizon crop\nreaches rgb(250, 240, 228) and the horizontal scrim thins to 0.35 exactly\nthere.\n\nTwo changes, together, because neither is enough alone. The ink ladder's alpha\nsteps were measured against the flat card #08080b and do not survive a\nphotograph, so hero text that carries meaning is full ivory; and the scrim's\nmiddle stop goes 0.35 to 0.58. 0.58 is the measured floor, not a guess: it is\nthe smallest value that clears every hero line once the text is full ivory,\nand it still opens the middle enough for the horizon to read. Worst line is\nnow the quiet link at 4.99, the dateline 12.62, the facts line 7.75.\n\nFound while doing WP-G's shadow reduction, which made it visible: the hero\ntext-shadows drop from 24px/12px at 60% to 2px at 30-35%, and that shadow had\nbeen quietly propping up text the scrim should have been carrying. The CTA's\ndrop shadow goes from 0 8px 32px at 40% to 0 6px 20px at 30%, and the quiet\nlink's underline from 0.35 to 0.55, since the underline is the only thing\nmarking it as a link.\n\nAlso WP-G's label cull. Today printed the date a second time, three lines\nunder the hero's own dateline and in the same mono treatment; it is gone. The\nReady / New chips inside the Today rails were mono uppercase micro-labels on a\ncontrol, and the label voice is for facts on a surface: they are sentence case\nnow, which is what the data always said.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1", "description": "Commit WP-G"}
