## Words never to use in student copy

From the glossary. Internal names may stay in code, never on the page.

| Never | Use instead |
|---|---|
| beat, beats | step |
| chunk | piece |
| intake | plain instruction |
| briefing, phase, tab, card | the thing's own name |
| say it back | tell it back |
| The Read | The Recall |
| Recall Rephrase Colour | Read, Recall, Colour |
| em dash, en dash | a full stop, or a colon before a list |

Related: [[teaching-voice]] · [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]] · [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]

  const startFallback = section === "rc" ? onStartSmartRc : startSmartDrill;

  /* The hero leads with the computed next step when there is one. Assigned

  const primary = !focus.loading && !focus.hasError ? focus.primary : null;
  const NextIcon = primary?.Icon;

  return (
    /* Surface, scrim and grid live in .drill-observatory-hero / -hero-grid: the
       horizon image needs image-set() and a two-layer scrim pseudo-element,
       neither of which belongs in a className. */
                 Today resolves. */
              <div className="drill-observatory-next animate-pulse" aria-label="Loading today">
                <div className="drill-observatory-skeleton-bar h-2.5 w-28" />
                <div className="drill-observatory-skeleton-bar mt-2 h-5 w-64 max-w-full" />
                <div className="drill-observatory-skeleton-bar mt-1 h-3 w-20" />
              </div>
            ) : primary ? (

                  {["Next up", primary.sourceLine, primary.status, primary.actionCue].filter(Boolean).join(" · ")}

              </div>

                {section === "rc"
                  ? "Read one passage now, untimed, with the answers after it. Or build a set of passages around exactly what you want to read."
                  : "Start an adaptive set that follows your weakest question types, or build a drill around exactly what you want to practice."}
              </p>
            )}
          </div>

          <div className="drill-observatory-cta-row">
            {primary ? (
              <>
                <Button
                  onClick={() => void focus.open(primary)}
                  disabled={!primary.route && !primary.onOpen}
                  className="drill-observatory-cta w-fit"
                >
                  {actionText(primary)}

=== head ===
# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.

## user

### "PT105.S1 · 24 stimuli · about 25 min" → removed
The section was already the second line of the title. The clock is at the foot
and in the long version. The stimulus count measured the work in a unit she has
no feel for.
**Lesson:** delete a line that repeats what is above it. Do not reword it.

## Booking emails: hand over the link, not the pass settings (2026-09-08)

**Cut:** "70 minutes each, and no expiration date, so there's no rush to use them."
**Keep:** "You're set up on LSAT U with 10 sessions."

Session length and expiry are admin fields. The student sees them on the booking
page when she picks a time, so putting them in the email is detail she did not ask
for and has to read past to reach the link. The credit count is worth saying because
it tells her how much she has. Everything else about the pass stays in the backend.

**Lesson:** in a booking email, say who it's for, how many sessions, and the link.
Stop there.

## user

Tool result:
/**
 * Which section of the drill hub a student is looking at.
 *
 * The hub is one page with two sections, LR and RC, chosen by the toggle in
 * its tools row. Section is URL state (`?section=rc`), not component state, so
 * a refresh, a Back press, or a shared link all land where the student was.
 * The last section is remembered per browser and applied ONLY when the URL is
 * silent — a deep link always outranks the remembered value.
 */
export type DrillSection = "lr" | "rc";

export const DRILL_SECTION_PARAM = "section";
export const DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY = "lsatu.drill.section";

/** `rc` and nothing else. Anything unrecognised is LR, never an error. */
export function parseDrillSection(value: string | null | undefined): DrillSection | null {
  if (value == null) return null;
  const v = value.trim().toLowerCase();
  if (v === "rc") return "rc";
  if (v === "lr") return "lr";
  return null;
}

/**
 * Storage is wrapped because it throws outright in Safari private mode and in
 * a sandboxed iframe — a remembered preference is never worth a blank page.
 */
export function readRememberedSection(): DrillSection | null {
  try {
    return parseDrillSection(window.localStorage.getItem(DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY));
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

export function rememberSection(section: DrillSection): void {
  try {
    window.localStorage.setItem(DRILL_SECTION_STORAGE_KEY, section);
  } catch {
    /* no-op: see readRememberedSection */
  }
}

/**
 * The one resolution rule: an explicit `?section=` wins, then the remembered
 * value, then LR. A student who has never chosen lands on LR every time — the
 * toggle is the discovery, and the page never moves under them.
 *
 * "Present but unrecognised" is NOT the same as absent. A link that says
 * `?section=sideways` has stated a section, badly; honouring the browser's
 * remembered RC there would open a section the link did not ask for. Any
 * stated section that is not RC resolves to LR, which is what the spec says
 * and what a malformed link should get.
 */
export function resolveDrillSection(param: string | null | undefined): DrillSection {
  const stated = param != null && param.trim() !== "";
  if (stated) return parseDrillSection(param) ?? "lr";
  return readRememberedSection() ?? "lr";
}

export const DRILL_SECTION_LABELS: Record<DrillSection, { short: string; full: string; route: string }> = {
  lr: { short: "LR", full: "Logical Reasoning", route: "DRILL / LOGICAL REASONING" },
  rc: { short: "RC", full: "Reading Comprehension", route: "DRILL / READING COMPREHENSION" },
};

export default function QuestionFinder({ returnTo, tone = "light", bank }: QuestionFinderProps) {
  const [open, setOpen] = React.useState(false);

  /* Cmd/Ctrl+K is an accelerator on top of a visible, labelled control — never
     the only way in. It is ignored while the student is typing somewhere else,
     so it cannot steal a keystroke from a real field. */
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (event.key.toLowerCase() !== "k" || !(event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey)) return;
      const target = event.target as HTMLElement | null;
      const tag = target?.tagName;
      if (!open && (tag === "INPUT" || tag === "TEXTAREA" || target?.isContentEditable)) return;
      event.preventDefault();
      setOpen((value) => !value);
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [open]);

  return (
    <div className="qf" data-tone={tone}>
      {/* ONE trigger, not one per breakpoint. Two buttons behind a CSS
          display swap would both stay in the accessibility tree, and a screen
          reader would announce "Find a question" twice on every visit. The
          label is what collapses on a phone; the control and its accessible
          name do not change. The shortcut still works but is not advertised
          on the trigger: Joshua's call, 2026-09-08. */}
      <button
        type="button"
        className="qf-trigger"

{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\pages\\AcademyMainhub.tsx", "old_string": "  // The analytics stage takes over the below-hero area; suppress mainhub\n  // overlays (chat widget) the same way an expanded orbital node used to.\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    setOrbitalActive(stage !== \"default\");\n    return () => setOrbitalActive(false);\n  }, [setOrbitalActive, stage]);", "new_string": "  /* Escape leaves a stage. The stage is a room the hub opened, and every other\n     room in this app closes on Escape; the Cancel button stays the visible way\n     out. Deliberately narrow about when it fires:\n\n     - only when a stage is actually open, so it never swallows Escape on the\n       plain hub,\n     - never when something modal is open (the search panel, a Radix popover or\n       select). Those close themselves on Escape, and closing the stage out\n       from under them would take two rooms away for one keypress,\n     - never out of a field the student has typed into, where Escape is the\n       browser's own revert. An empty field is fine to escape from. */\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    if (stage === \"default\") return;\n    const onKey = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {\n      if (event.key !== \"Escape\" || event.defaultPrevented) return;\n      if (document.querySelector('[role=\"dialog\"], [data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]')) return;\n      const target = event.target as HTMLElement | null;\n      const tag = target?.tagName;\n      if (tag === \"INPUT\" || tag === \"TEXTAREA\" || target?.isContentEditable === true) {\n        const value = (target as HTMLInputElement).value;\n        if (value != null && value !== \"\") return;\n      }\n      closeStage();\n    };\n    window.addEventListener(\"keydown\", onKey);\n    return () => window.removeEventListener(\"keydown\", onKey);\n  }, [closeStage, stage]);\n\n  // The analytics stage takes over the below-hero area; suppress mainhub\n  // overlays (chat widget) the same way an expanded orbital node used to.\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    setOrbitalActive(stage !== \"default\");\n    return () => setOrbitalActive(false);\n  }, [setOrbitalActive, stage]);"}

function renderHub(entry: string) {
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  return render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <MemoryRouter
        initialEntries={[entry]}
        future={{ v7_relativeSplatPath: true, v7_startTransition: true }}
      >
        <AcademyMainhub />
        <UrlProbe />
        <BackButton />
      </MemoryRouter>
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
}

const url = () => new URLSearchParams(screen.getByTestId("url").textContent ?? "");
const lrSwitch = () => screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Logical Reasoning" });
const rcSwitch = () => screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Reading Comprehension" });

describe("AcademyMainhub — the LR/RC section switch", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    window.HTMLElement.prototype.scrollIntoView = vi.fn();
    window.localStorage.clear();
  });
=== tail ===

  it("keeps section choice in the LR/RC switch instead of repeating it as a practice tile", () => {
    renderHub("/classroom/drill");
    expect(
      screen.queryByRole("button", {
        name: "Reading Comprehension Drill full passages the way they appear on the test.",
      }),
    ).toBeNull();

    fireEvent.click(rcSwitch());
    expect(url().get("section")).toBe("rc");
    expect(screen.getByRole("region", { name: "Practice and insight workspace" })).toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

Today is assembled from seven sources and only two are react-query backed, so invalidating queries would retry two of seven and look like it had retried all of them. ): TodayFocusItem | null {
  if (!row) return null;
  const snapshot = row.state_snapshot;
  const target = resolveResumeTarget(snapshot);
  const label = labelFor(snapshot);
  if (snapshot.kind === "bootcamp_step") {
    return {
      id: `last-${row.activity_id}`,
      type: "bootcamp",
      title: label,
      sourceLine: "Bootcamp progress",
      status: "In progress",
      actionLabel: "Continue",
      route: target.path,
      routeState: target.state,
      priority: 520,
      updatedAt: row.updated_at,
      Icon: BookOpenCheck,
    };
  }

  const total = snapshot.qids.length;
  const left = Math.max(0, total - snapshot.current_index);
  const progress = total > 0 ? `${Math.min(100, Math.round((snapshot.current_index / total) * 100))}%` : undefined;
  return {
    id: `last-${row.activity_id}`,
    type: snapshot.kind === "section" ? "set" : "drill",
    title: label,
    sourceLine: snapshot.kind === "section" ? "Active practice set" : "Unfinished drill",
    status: "In progress",
    actionLabel: "Resume",
    actionCue: left > 0 ? `${left} left` : undefined,
    route: target.path,
    routeState:
      target.path === "/drill"
        ? { ...(target.state as object), returnTo: drillReturnTo }
        : target.state,
    priority: snapshot.kind === "section" ? 480 : 460,
    progress,

## assistant

Everything is pushed and the report is written. Here's where the job landed.

## Done and published (verified on production)

**WP-A/B, C, D, E, motion.** The two that mattered most:

`13bb1dd` shipped a regression that **every scan passed**. WP-C added the phone menu bar as a third child of `.classroom-root`, which is a flex *row* — so the bar took a column of its own and squeezed every classroom section. Workspace was down to 181px of 375. Nothing overflowed, so contrast, targets, overflow and heading scans all came back clean; only the screenshot sweep caught it. Fixed in `a82333a` and re-verified across all five sections.

**WP-E took `!important` from 200 to 4**, verified in the built bundle rather than just the source. The four that remain are the `prefers-reduced-motion` block — that `!important` is the switch that turns animation off for someone who asked their OS for less motion, and it has to beat a later animation rule. I did not trade that for a round number.

Since screenshots of a scrolled page come back blank in this pane, I proved "the page looks identical" with a 200-element × 50-property fingerprint diffed before and after publish. It changed exactly three things, all of them the authored rules finally beating Tailwind utilities.

## Done and pushed, not yet published

`afb15a3`, `6109c2f`, `daa41d8` — WP-G, H, I, J, K, L, M, N. **Publish when you can; none of it is measured on the live build yet.**

The find worth your attention: **the contrast scan has been reporting zero fails while three hero lines were failing.** It reads the scrim's declared colour, not the photograph under it. Sampling the actual webp pixel-by-pixel under each text box:

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| "Start a Smart Drill instead" | **1.89:1** | 4.99 |
| Next up facts line | **2.76:1** | 7.75 |
| Dateline | **4.50:1** | 12.62 |

The ink ladder's alpha steps were measured against the flat card, and they don't survive a photograph. Hero text is full ivory now and the scrim's middle stop went 0.35 → 0.58, which is the measured floor, not a guess.

## Four places the plan was wrong

I verified each before setting it aside rather than implementing it blindly.

**WP-F I did not ship.** Its premise is false: `ThemeContext` adds `.light` and removes it, so dark is the *absence* of a class, while `classroom.css` keys its whole dark palette on `html.dark` — which nothing in the app ever sets. Twenty-two dark rules are dead code. Adding dark stage tokens under `html.dark`, as WP-F instructs, would have been dead on arrival too. I simulated the real fix on production to confirm the diagnosis. But the correct fix changes the selector for the entire classroom, all five sections, for every student in dark theme, and it has never once been rendered. That is its own session with its own verification pass, not a line item inside a drill-hub polish plan. **That's your call to make, not mine to slip in.**

The others: WP-K's disabled-CTA "trap" is an unreachable state (`resolveItemRoute` returns a string on every branch); WP-J's "define a scale" was already done at `classroom.css:954` and its proposed values would have moved every size on the page; and the adaptive drill has no fixed ten-question set, so the explainer quotes no number.

## Scores

Audit **16/20 → 21/25**, critique **31/40 → 42/50** (80%→84% and 78%→84% like-for-like). Every row has evidence tied to a measurement; Performance stays at **3** because post-preload LCP could not be measured and a 3 without evidence stays a 3.

## Two things I owe you plainly

**I broke your push rule before you gave it.** My earlier push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, carrying another session's commit `70056d3` with mine. That same session's broad `git add` had also swept my motion edits into *their* commit, so `70056d3` contains work its message doesn't mention. Both facts are in the report so the log doesn't mislead later. Since then I've checked `git log origin/main..HEAD` before every push and only pushed my own.

**Performance and WP-F are the two open threads.** The LCP trace needs you signed into the chrome-devtools browser in the same sitting as the trace, since it loses the profile on restart.

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **WP-E: `!important` 200 → 4**, via `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\scripts\observatory-specificity.mjs` (postcss, idempotent, `--check` guard). Four kept in `prefers-reduced-motion` deliberately: that `!important` is the off-switch for motion-sensitive users and must beat a later animation rule. Verified in the built bundle, not just source.
- **`.drill-observatory-launcher-panel` deliberately excluded** via a `/* end observatory */` marker — it lands on Radix portal content outside the root; scoping it would unstyle every dropdown.
- **WP-C regression found and fixed (`a82333a`).** `13bb1dd` added the phone bar as a third child of `.classroom-root`, a flex *row*, squeezing all five classroom sections (Workspace to 181px of 375). Every scan passed; only the screenshot sweep caught it. Bar needs `flex: none` (Messages shrank it to 52px).
- **Three hero WCAG failures found that the 1a scan structurally cannot see** — it reads the scrim's declared colour, not the photograph. Measured by canvas-sampling `hub-horizon.webp`: quiet link 1.89:1, Next up facts 2.76:1, dateline 4.50:1. Fixed by full-ivory hero text plus scrim mid-stop 0.35 → **0.58** (binary-searched floor). Now 4.99 / 7.75 / 12.62.
- **Motion easing corrected** — `--observatory-motion` was Material ease-in-out; at 10% of 160ms it had covered 2.6% of distance. Now `--observatory-ease` quart (34.7%). Also caught that WP-E would have made three hovers snap (transition lists named only `transform` while `:hover` changed `background`).
- **WP-F NOT shipped.** Premise false: `ThemeContext` adds/removes `.light`, so dark is the *absence* of a class, while `classroom.css` keys 22 dark rules on `html.dark` which nothing sets. Fix is whole-classroom, never rendered, needs its own session.
- **Copy pass logged** to `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\wiki\COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` under a 2026-09-09 heading so it cannot be silently reverted.
